● Both codebases explored. Here's the brief:

  prefrontal-mw (backend)

  .NET 10 MVP scaffold, ~18.5K LOC, single project (not yet sharded into 
  Modules/Lms/Hms).

  - Layout: Controllers/ (16) → Services/ (12) → Repositories/ (12) → Models/
  (37 entities) → Data/AppDbContext + 3 migrations (all Apr 18–19 — schema still
   churning).
  - Domain: Orders (with package→child-test explosion), Samples (barcoded
  PF-{vendor}-{year}-{seq}-{dept}), Results (versioned), Bills (full
  CGST/SGST/IGST + HSN/SAC + interstate detection), Reports (QR tokens),
  Patients (ABHA/Aadhaar fields — not wired), RBAC (Tenants→Branches→Regions,
  wildcard perms).
  - Auth: Supabase JWT validator is configured (Program.cs:98) but bypassed —
  UserContextMiddleware.cs:19 hard-codes an Admin/"Apollo Diagnostics"/"Chennai
  Main" user with "*" perms. Must flip before prod.
  - Dynamic workflows: form schemas stored as JSONB in workflow_configs,
  re-seeded on SchemaVersion bump.
  - Gaps: no payment gateway, no SMS/WhatsApp/email sender, ABHA/Aadhaar APIs
  not called, no tests, placeholder GST state "TN" in billing.

  prefrontal-ui (frontend)

  Nx monorepo, single lms app, Angular 21 zoneless + signals, Tailwind v4. 
  Early-to-mid WIP, no shared libs yet.

  - Routes (app.routes.ts, lazy, role-based): /admin, /reception, /lab,
  /pathologist, /reports, /dashboard. Auth guard is commented out.
  - Auth (core/auth/auth.service.ts): Supabase session → signals (_session,
  _user, isAuthenticated, accessToken); hasPermission() with * wildcard;
  auth.interceptor.ts attaches Bearer to /api/*. Dev env uses auth.bypass: true
  with a MOCK_USER.
  - API (ApiService): wraps HttpClient with environment.apiUrl =
  http://localhost:5270 (points at prefrontal-mw); unwraps { success, data, 
  error }; exposes isLoading/error signals.
  - UI system: no PrimeNG/Material — custom primitives (avatar, chip, tile,
  segmented, toggle, touch-button, etc.), glassmorphic tokens in styles.scss,
  ambient DNA/helix background, mobile bottom-nav + desktop sidebar, 44px tap
  targets, view-transition theme fade.
  - Signals usage: signal()/asReadonly()/computed() consistent, OnPush
  everywhere. No resource() or linkedSignal() yet — natural next step for data
  fetching.
  - Gaps: most feature screens are skeleton-heavy, no E2E tests (only
  example.spec.ts), no i18n, no PWA, no state lib.

  Overall: backend and frontend line up cleanly — same domain model (orders,
  samples, bills, reports, role gates) and both share the same "JWT built, auth
  bypassed for dev" state. Next sensible milestones: turn auth on end-to-end,
  wire a payment gateway + SMS sender on the backend, and flesh out the
  reception/lab/pathologist workflows on the UI.

  Structurally the shell is already identical (sticky pill top + floating pill
  bottom + ambient canvas). So this is really a re-skin job.

  Proposed plan — 3 focused phases

  Phase 1 — Tokens & palette (the biggest visual win, ~1 file)
  Rewrite styles.scss color block: swap --gradient-primary, --bg-primary,
  shadow/glow/hover tokens, and the :root.light counterparts to teal/mint + ink.
   Everything using var(--gradient-primary) / var(--bg-*) re-skins automatically
   — that includes buttons, badges, avatars, nav active states.

  Phase 2 — Layout alignment (top-strip + bottom-nav + ambient-bg)
  - Move the branch selector to the left (right after the brand lockup),
  matching the screenshot.
  - Change nav active state from gradient pill → white pill with ink text (just
  two class changes).
  - Add Order and Billing to PRIMARY_NAV; trim MORE_NAV to match the reference's
   lean set.
  - Recolor ambient-bg blobs teal/mint (or add a mode flag so light theme keeps
  its own palette).

  Phase 3 — Brand lockup choice (ask first)
  The reference uses a tiny teal→mint circular badge with a single ECG stroke —
  much quieter than the current animated "PREFRONTAL · Lab Management System"
  wordmark. Two options:
  - (a) Recolor existing — keep the animated brand lockup, just repaint its
  gradients teal/mint. Low risk, keeps the personality.
  - (b) Simplify to match — replace with the tiny circle + "Prefrontal / time ·
  LMS" two-line badge. More faithful to the reference but throws away work you
  already have.

  Main tradeoff: a straight recolor (Phase 1+2a) gets you ~90% of the "feel" in
  maybe 30 minutes of edits and stays reversible through the CSS variables. Full
   structural match (adding Phase 2's nav changes + Phase 3b) is another ~1 hour
   and changes a bit of muscle memory for anyone already using the app.

  Want me to:
  1. Start with Phase 1 only (token swap) so you can see the impact and then
  decide on structure?
  2. Do Phase 1 + 2 together (recommended — it's what "layout + theme" really
  means)?
  3. Go all the way through Phase 3b for a pixel-faithful port?

  Tell me which, and I'll execute. I'd also suggest I start the Nx dev server
  after each phase so you can see it live in the browser.

  The "touch + web" angle

  The reference is already one responsive source: grid grid-cols-1 
  lg:grid-cols-[1fr_420px] stacks on tablet, splits on desktop. NumPad is
  visible on touch devices and harmlessly usable on desktop. That means one 
  implementation per screen covers both — no separate .touch.ts vs .web.ts. For
  value entry specifically I'll wire each numeric parameter to both a text input
   (pf-number-input) and open an optional slide-up NumPad on tap — keyboard
  folks ignore it, touch folks love it.

  Important thing to decide before I code

  The current billing, order-create, and result-entry all run through
  <pf-workflow-renderer>, which is a backend-driven generic form (schema in
  workflow_configs table, re-seeded by SchemaVersion). To match the reference
  I'd replace the workflow renderer on these three screens with domain-specific
  components. Pros: far richer UX matching the reference. Cons: the workflow
  JSON configs for billing / order_creation / result_entry become unused (the
  configs still exist on the backend, just not rendered here). Your call — two
  options:

  - (A) Replace workflow-renderer on these screens → full reference-style UX,
  cleanest code
  - (B) Wrap workflow-renderer → keep JSON-driven, layer extra UI around it
  (banner, sticky bar, cart, scan bar). More compromise but preserves
  configurability.

  I'd go (A) for result-entry and billing (both are specialized and the rich UX
  matters); (B) for order-create is tempting since the workflow form is already
  pretty flexible, but the cart experience is hard to bolt on — probably (A)
  there too.

  Proposed stages (each independently shippable)

  1. pf-num-pad primitive (~150 lines) — 3×4 touch keypad, reusable everywhere
  2. Result Entry — 3-col param grid, live flags, critical banner, sticky bottom
   bar, optional NumPad slide-up
  3. Billing — invoice tile + payment-mode grid + UPI/Card/Cash per-mode panels
  (Cash = NumPad + quick-add + live change)
  4. Order Create — patient chip + priority pills + package cards + test catalog
   toggle + sticky cart
  5. Lab / My Work — big scan bar + counter tile row (keep the urgency buckets
  below)
  6. Reception landing — light polish (KPI row + segmented time filter) — only
  if needed after reading it

  A heads-up

  Backend's still not running (Postgres isn't up on :5432). That means the new
  screens will render with empty data until you start Postgres and re-run dotnet
   run. I can build against the current data shapes (OrderTest, Bill, Test, etc.
   — all already modelled) so it'll light up the moment the DB is back.

  ---
  Tell me:
  1. Stage scope — all 5 (minus 6 unless needed), or just result-entry + billing
   first?
  2. Replace workflow-renderer (A) or wrap it (B) on result-entry / billing /
  order-create?
  3. Want me to also try to help get Postgres running (docker one-liner or
  explore your docker setup)?

  Once you say, I'll build — starting with the NumPad primitive since every
  other stage leans on it.

      379 +    const enriched = this.enrich(this._rows());               
      380 +    switch (id) {                                             
      381 +      case 'pending':     return enriched.filter(t => t.status
          + === 'pending' || t.status === 're_entry').length;            
      382 +      case 'critical':    return enriched.filter(t => this.isC
          +ritical(t)).length;                                           
      383 +      case 'in_progress': return enriched.filter(t => t.status
          + === 'in_progress' || t.status === 'entered').length;         
      384 +      case 'done':        return enriched.filter(t => t.status
          + === 'validated' || t.status === 'signed_off').length;        
      385 +      default:            return enriched.length;             
      386 +    }                                                         
      387 +  }                                                           
      388 +                                                              
      389 +  private isCritical(t: OrderTest): boolean {                 
      390 +    if (!t.currentResult) return false;                       
      391 +    try {                                                     
      392 +      const snap = JSON.parse(t.currentResult) as { flags?: { 
          +critical?: boolean } };                                       
      393 +      return snap.flags?.critical === true;                   
      394 +    } catch { return false; }                                 
      395 +  }                                                           
      396 +

           mber' : 'default';
      399    }
     ...
      430      return true;
      431    }

      433 +  /** Try to resolve a scanned barcode/OrderTest id → jump to 
          +result entry. */

          +d and barcode).

      439 +      r.id === q ||

      441 +      r.subOrder?.id === q                                    
      442 +    );

      444 +    // Otherwise try a direct fetch by id.                    
      445 +    this.api.get<OrderTest>(`/api/results/${q}`).subscribe({  
      446 +      next: t => { if (t) this.enter(t); else this.toasts.warn
          +ing('No match for scan'); },                                  
      447 +      error: () => this.toasts.warning('No match for scan')   
      448 +    });

  - Payment mode grid (3-col fixed)
  - Form-field pair grids in admin editors — 2 columns is the readable max for
  paired inputs
  - Action button pairs in patient-profile (New order / other action)
  - 12-col and custom [Xpx_Yfr] grids — those are layout utilities, not card
  lists

  Refresh and the wider your screen, the more cards per row. Let me know if any
  specific screen still feels empty and I'll tune it.

● Clean. Refresh the result-entry page. What you'll see:

  Layout changed to [main | 340px sticky NumPad rail] on desktop. On mobile the
  rail hides — native keyboard handles numeric input there.

  NumPad rail (right side, sticky):
  - Always visible, always usable
  - Shows a live target header — which param you're editing + current value +
  unit
  - Keypad routes its presses to the active param (no per-row toggle anymore)
  - Next empty button jumps focus to the next empty numeric field
  - Clear wipes the active value
  - Keyboard hint at the bottom (Tab / Enter)

  The "cool" bits:
  - Active param row glows — violet/pink tinted background, left border accent,
  lifted shadow, the index badge flips to gradient fill. Makes it obvious which
  row the pad is driving
  - Progress bar in the Results tile header showing filled / required as a
  gradient fill
  - Critical flag pill pulses — subtle breathing shadow on any critical tone
  badge
  - Flag pill slide-in animation — each flag badge fades up smoothly the moment
  a value crosses the threshold
  - Numbered index pills on each param, which also flip to gradient-fill on the
  active row
  - Numeric inputs grow taller (52px, 20px tabular-nums font) — much more
  readable

  Keyboard flow that still works:
  - Tab/Shift-Tab moves between fields natively
  - Enter on a numeric or text input jumps to the next empty one (any param
  type, any previous or future)
  - Typing keys hit the input directly; the pad mirrors the value in real-time
  - inputmode="decimal" still brings up numeric soft-keyboard on mobile

  Try typing in one field (keyboard) and tapping the numpad on another. The
  target header follows whichever input you focus.

● Compile clean. Refresh — the page is now locked to the viewport:

  What changed:
  - html, body, .shell-stack all locked at 100dvh (dynamic viewport height —
  mobile URL bar aware), overflow: hidden
  - <main> is a flex child that fills leftover space between top-strip and
  bottom-nav, overflow: hidden
  - The routed feature's Angular host (e.g. <pf-result-entry>,
  <pf-order-create>) is now height: 100%; overflow-y: auto — this is the single
  scroll container per page

  Net effect:
  - The page as a whole doesn't scroll (no browser chrome scrollbar)
  - Each feature's content scrolls internally when it overflows
  - The top-strip stays fixed at top, the bottom-nav stays fixed at bottom, both
   always visible
  - Sticky elements inside a feature (like result-entry's right-rail NumPad
  sticky top-24, or order-create's lg:sticky lg:top-24 cart) continue to stick
  because they're now sticky relative to the feature's scroll container, not the
   page

  Things to watch for:
  - The result-entry sticky bottom action bar uses position: fixed — still
  anchored to viewport, still visible.
  - Any feature that forgot to add internal padding will have content reach the
  edges — holler if a specific screen looks cramped.
  - On very short viewports (< ~600px tall), dense screens like admin-dashboard
  or my-work will scroll within themselves — which is exactly what you want.

  Poke around and let me know which screen feels off and I'll tune.

● Pushed. Refresh the browser (⌘/Ctrl+R — hot-reload alone may not re-cascade
  the root-level CSS chain).

  What was missing: <app-root> (Angular's bootstrap element) and <pf-shell> (its
   child after the router-outlet) were both inline-by-default, so body { height:
   100dvh } couldn't propagate. Content overflowed the body, so the browser gave
   you a page scroll. Now:

  html (100dvh, overflow:hidden)
    body (100dvh, overflow:hidden)
      app-root (block, height:100%)            ← newly fixed
        pf-shell (block, height:100%)          ← newly fixed via `router-outlet
  + *`
          .shell-stack (height:100%, flex col)
            pf-top-strip (sticky)
            main.shell-main (flex:1, overflow:hidden)
              pf-feature (block, height:100%, overflow-y:auto)  ← THE scroll
  happens here
            pf-bottom-nav (fixed)

  Page shouldn't scroll anywhere; only the feature host inside <main> does. If
  you still see a page scrollbar, open DevTools and tell me which element shows
  a scrollbar — there may be a stray feature with a hard min-height pushing past
   viewport.

● Clean. Refresh the iPad Mini view.

  What you should see now:
  - NumPad rail visible on iPad and above (lg: ≥ 1024px). At iPad width it takes
   320px; at desktop it grows to 360px
  - Sticky action bar (Reject / Save draft / Submit) now properly pinned to the
  bottom of the viewport — the will-change: transform that Angular's page-enter
  animation was setting was creating a new containing block and making the fixed
   bar float with the scroll. Killed it.
  - Param rows are still stacked at iPad width (label + ref on top → input →
  flag), which gives the input full-width instead of getting squeezed into a
  240px column next to a chunky rail
  - On desktop xl: (≥ 1280px), rows flip to the 3-column layout and everything
  fits side by side

  On the numpad itself — keys are 64px tall, full-width rail, numbers are 22px
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums. With the rail padded 18px and 8px key
  gaps, individual keys come out at ~85px square on iPad. That's
  finger-comfortable. Let me know if you want bigger/smaller keys or different
  border radius for more "crisp".

● The action bar landing in the middle is a classic Chrome bug: the page-enter
  animation's translateY(0) end state creates a new containing block, breaking
  position: fixed. Better fix than chasing that: stop using position: fixed
  entirely and restructure result-entry as a proper flex-column page where the
  params list is the only thing that scrolls, and the action bar is just the
  page's flex footer.

● Clean compile. Refresh — the result-entry page is now structured as:

  ┌──────────────────── top strip (shell) ───────────────────┐
  ├──────────────────── ScreenHeader (static) ───────────────┤
  │                                                           │
  │ ┌── patient + TAT (static) ───────────┬───────────────┐  │
  │ ├── (critical banner if any, static) ─┤               │  │
  │ │                                     │               │  │
  │ │ ┌ Results ──────────────────────┐   │  NumPad rail  │  │
  │ │ │ header + progress (static)    │   │  (static,     │  │
  │ │ │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │   │   always      │  │
  │ │ │ │ param 1                   │ │   │   visible)    │  │
  │ │ │ │ param 2    ◀── SCROLLS ──▶│ │   │               │  │
  │ │ │ │ param 3                   │ │   │               │  │
  │ │ │ │ ...                       │ │   │               │  │
  │ │ │ └───────────────────────────┘ │   │               │  │
  │ │ └───────────────────────────────┘   │               │  │
  │ │                                     │               │  │
  │ ├── notes (static) ───────────────────┤               │  │
  │ └─────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────┘  │
  │                                                           │
  ├──────────────────── action bar (static footer) ──────────┤
  └──────────────────── bottom nav pill ─────────────────────┘

  What's fixed:
  - The action bar is now a proper flex footer — not position: fixed, so no more
   landing in the middle of the viewport. It sits at the true bottom of the
  page, above the bottom-nav
  - Only the params list scrolls internally. Patient tile, critical banner,
  notes, and the action bar all stay put
  - NumPad rail stays always visible, aligned beside the scrolling params —
  matches your "values | numpad" requirement
  - Global pageSlideIn animation changed to opacity-only so no stray
  translateY(0) creates a new containing block

  Scroll through the params and you'll see only the list moves; everything else
  — patient, progress bar, numpad, submit buttons — stays locked in place.
